G_{n}^{\tau}(z)=A_{n}^{\tau}(z)+B_{n}^{\tau}(z)+\frac{1}{2}\left((A_{n}^{\tau}(z))^{2}+(B_{n}^{\tau}(z))^{2}\right)+r_{n}^{\tau}(z),

where

|r_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C\left(|A_{n}^{\tau}(z)|^{3}+|B_{n}^{\tau}(z)|^{3}\right)\leq C\varepsilon_{\tau}^{3}

uniformly for
0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
and
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
.

The linear term is

A_{n}^{\tau}(z)+B_{n}^{\tau}(z)=U_{n}^{\tau}(z)-zd_{\tau}.

For the quadratic term, a direct computation gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left((A_{n}^{\tau}(z))^{2}+(B_{n}^{\tau}(z))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left((z(c_{\tau}-d_{\tau}))^{2}+z^{2}c_{\tau}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{z}{1+\beta}\left((c_{\tau}-d_{\tau})-\beta c_{\tau}\right)U_{n}^{\tau}(z)+\frac{1+\beta^{2}}{2(1+\beta)^{2}}\left(U_{n}^{\tau}(z)\right)^{2}.

Since
d_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{2})
and
c_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau})
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\left((z(c_{\tau}-d_{\tau}))^{2}+z^{2}c_{\tau}^{2}\right)=c_{\tau}^{2}z^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{3}),

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, and

\frac{z}{1+\beta}\left((c_{\tau}-d_{\tau})-\beta c_{\tau}\right)=\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}\,c_{\tau}z+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{2})

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
.
Moreover, by Proposition
B.1
,

U_{n}^{\tau}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau})

uniformly on
\{0,\dots,N_{\tau}\}\times\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, and thus the
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{2})

coefficient error in the cross term contributes only
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{3})
.
Collecting the terms proves (
3.23
)–(
3.24
).

B.6
Equivalent resolvent reformulation

The three lemmas in this subsection are algebraic rewrites. Lemma
B.2

replaces the averaged state variable by the current state up to a lower-order
error. Lemma
B.3
rewrites the discrete recursion in
resolvent form, and Lemma
B.4
gives the analogous
continuous resolvent form. Together they reduce the comparison to matching the
two resolvent kernels.

Lemma B.2
.

With the convention that the empty sum is zero when
n=0
, uniformly for

0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
and
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
,

\left|H_{n}^{\tau}(z)-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}.

(B.19)

Consequently,

H_{n}^{\tau}(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}^{\tau}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)+\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z),

(B.20)

where

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-2\alpha}.

(B.21)

Proof.

We first treat the case
n=0
. Since

H_{0}^{\tau}(z)=\frac{\mu}{\theta\varepsilon_{\tau}}G_{0}^{\tau}(z),\qquad G_{0}^{\tau}(z)=e^{\Theta_{+}^{\tau}(z)}+e^{\Theta_{-}^{\tau}(z)}-2,

with
\Theta_{+}^{\tau}(z)=z(c_{\tau}-d_{\tau})
and
\Theta_{-}^{\tau}(z)=-zc_{\tau}
,
and since
c_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau})
,
d_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\tau}^{2})
,
the linear terms cancel and therefore

\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|G_{0}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\varepsilon_{\tau}^{2}.

Hence,

\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|H_{0}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\varepsilon_{\tau}\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}.

Thus (
B.19
) holds for
n=0
.

Now let
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
. By Proposition
B.1
and the rescaling
(
3.25
),

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|H_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}.

Hence,

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T},

since
\sum_{k\geq 1}\varphi_{k}=1
.

Subtracting
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)
from both sides of
(
3.36
) gives

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\tau}(z)-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=-(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)+\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)\right)+E_{n}^{\tau}(z).

Using
1-a_{\tau}=\gamma\tau^{-\alpha}
, the boundedness of the convolution term,
the boundedness of
F_{z}
on the relevant range, and (
3.37
),
we obtain (
B.19
).

Since
F_{z}
is quadratic in
x
and the relevant arguments remain uniformly
bounded on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
,
F_{z}
is uniformly Lipschitz on the relevant
range. Hence, by (
B.19
),

\left|F_{z}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)\right)-F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)-H_{n}^{\tau}(z)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}.

Multiplying by
\tau^{-\alpha}
and using

a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}^{\tau}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z),

we obtain (
B.20
) with

\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z):=E_{n}^{\tau}(z)+\tau^{-\alpha}\left[F_{z}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}H_{n-k}^{\tau}(z)\right)-F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))\right].

The bound (
B.21
) follows immediately.
∎

Lemma B.3

(Renewal inversion)
.

Let

\psi^{\tau}:=\sum_{m\geq 1}(\varphi^{\tau})^{*m}\qquad\text{with}\qquad\varphi^{\tau}=a_{\tau}\varphi

be the renewal kernel associated with
\varphi^{\tau}
, and define

\mathsf{S}_{k}^{\tau}:=\frac{\tau(1-a_{\tau})}{a_{\tau}}\psi_{k}^{\tau},\qquad k\geq 1.

(B.22)

Then (
B.20
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)

(B.23)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\tau^{-\alpha-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(H_{j}^{\tau}(z))+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z),

for every
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
.

Proof.

Equation (
B.20
) is a discrete renewal equation of the form

H^{\tau}=U^{\tau}+\varphi^{\tau}*H^{\tau},

where

U_{n}^{\tau}(z):=\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z).

Since
\|\varphi^{\tau}\|_{1}=a_{\tau}<1
, the associated renewal kernel

\psi^{\tau}:=\sum_{m\geq 1}(\varphi^{\tau})^{*m}

is well defined in
\ell^{1}
. Iterating the renewal equation and summing the
Neumann series yields

H^{\tau}=U^{\tau}+\psi^{\tau}*U^{\tau},

that is,

H_{n}^{\tau}(z)=U_{n}^{\tau}(z)+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}U_{j}^{\tau}(z),\qquad n\geq 1.

Substituting the definition of
U_{j}^{\tau}(z)
yields

H_{n}^{\tau}(z)=\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{j}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z)\right).

Using (
B.22
),

\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}=\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau},

we obtain (
B.23
).
∎

Lemma B.4

(Continuous resolvent form)
.

For each
z
in the admissible strip under consideration, the solution of the
continuous fractional Riccati equation admits the representation

h(t,z)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t-s)F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds,\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

(B.24)

Proof.

Starting from (
3.8
), we rewrite it as

h(t,z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(-\gamma h(s,z)+F_{z}(h(s,z))\right)\,ds.

Taking Laplace transforms gives

\widehat{h}(\lambda,z)=\lambda^{-\alpha}\left(-\gamma\widehat{h}(\lambda,z)+\mathcal{L}_{t}[F_{z}(h(\cdot,z))](\lambda)\right),

and hence,

\widehat{h}(\lambda,z)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\alpha}+\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{t}[F_{z}(h(\cdot,z))](\lambda).

Since

\mathcal{L}\!\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}f_{\alpha,\gamma}\right\}(\lambda)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\alpha}+\gamma},

the claim follows by Laplace inversion.
∎

B.7
Lemma
B.5

Lemma B.5

(Discrete cumulative renewal equation)
.

For all
n\geq 1
,

\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}=a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}_{n-k}^{\tau}+\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}.

(B.25)

Moreover,

\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}.

(B.26)

Proof.

Let

\Psi_{\tau}(q):=\sum_{n\geq 1}\psi_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}=\frac{a_{\tau}\Phi(q)}{1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q)},\qquad\Phi(q):=\sum_{m\geq 1}\varphi_{m}q^{m}.

Then

\sum_{n\geq 1}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\psi_{k}^{\tau}\right)q^{n}=\frac{\Psi_{\tau}(q)}{1-q}=\frac{a_{\tau}\Phi(q)}{(1-q)(1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q))}.

Multiplying by
(1-a_{\tau})/(\gamma a_{\tau})
, we obtain

\sum_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma}\,\frac{\Phi(q)}{(1-q)(1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q))}.

Hence,

(1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q))\sum_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma}\frac{\Phi(q)}{1-q}.

Comparing coefficients of
q^{n}
gives

\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}=a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}_{n-k}^{\tau}+\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}.

Since
(1-a_{\tau})/\gamma=\tau^{-\alpha}
, this proves
(
B.25
).

Because
\varphi_{k}\geq 0
,
a_{\tau}\in(0,1)
, and

0\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\leq 1
,
we obtain

\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}\leq a_{\tau}\max_{0\leq j\leq n-1}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\tau}+\tau^{-\alpha}.

Setting
M_{n}^{\tau}:=\max_{0\leq j\leq n}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\tau}
, we get

M_{n}^{\tau}\leq a_{\tau}M_{n-1}^{\tau}+\tau^{-\alpha}.

Since
M_{0}^{\tau}=0
, iteration yields

M_{n}^{\tau}\leq\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}a_{\tau}^{r}\leq\frac{\tau^{-\alpha}}{1-a_{\tau}}=\frac{1}{\gamma}.

This proves (
B.26
).
∎

B.8
Lemma
B.6

Lemma B.6

(Laplace representation)
.

Define the piecewise-constant interpolation

\mathcal{G}^{\tau}(t):=\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau},\qquad t\in[n/\tau,(n+1)/\tau).

Then, for any
z>0
,

\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma z}\,\frac{\hat{\varphi}(z/\tau)}{1-a_{\tau}\hat{\varphi}(z/\tau)}.

(B.27)

Proof.

Let

q:=e^{-z/\tau}\in(0,1).

Since
\mathcal{G}^{\tau}(t)=\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}
on
[n/\tau,(n+1)/\tau)
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}\int_{n/\tau}^{(n+1)/\tau}e^{-zt}\,dt=\frac{1-q}{z}\sum_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}.

Since
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\tau}=0
, Lemma
B.5
yields

\sum_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}=\sum_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}q^{n}=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma}\,\frac{\Phi(q)}{(1-q)(1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q))},

where
\Phi(q):=\sum_{m\geq 1}\varphi_{m}q^{m}
. Therefore,

\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma z}\,\frac{\Phi(q)}{1-a_{\tau}\Phi(q)}.

Since
\Phi(e^{-z/\tau})=\hat{\varphi}(z/\tau)
, this is exactly
(
B.27
).
∎

B.9
Lemma
B.7

Lemma B.7

(Laplace-domain consistency)
.

Let
K\subset(0,\infty)
be compact. Then uniformly for
z\in K
,

\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)-\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right),

where

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(z)=\frac{1}{z(\gamma+z^{\alpha})}.

Proof.

Set
s:=z/\tau
. By Lemma
3.2
,

\hat{\varphi}(s)=1-s^{\alpha}-c_{1}(\alpha)s+\mathcal{O}(s^{1+\alpha}),

uniformly for
z\in K
as
\tau\to\infty
, because
K
is compact. Therefore

\displaystyle 1-a_{\tau}\hat{\varphi}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-a_{\tau})+a_{\tau}(1-\hat{\varphi}(s))

\displaystyle=\gamma\tau^{-\alpha}+a_{\tau}z^{\alpha}\tau^{-\alpha}+a_{\tau}c_{1}(\alpha)z\tau^{-1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1-\alpha})

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\alpha}\left(\gamma+z^{\alpha}+c_{1}(\alpha)z\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})\right),

again uniformly for
z\in K
. Since
K\subset(0,\infty)
, the quantity

\gamma+z^{\alpha}
is bounded away from
0
on
K
. Therefore, for
\tau
large
enough,

\left|1-a_{\tau}\hat{\varphi}(z/\tau)\right|\geq c_{K}\tau^{-\alpha}

for some
c_{K}>0
.
Using Lemma
B.6
,

\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\gamma z}\,\frac{\hat{\varphi}(s)}{1-a_{\tau}\hat{\varphi}(s)}=\frac{\hat{\varphi}(s)}{z\left(\gamma+z^{\alpha}+c_{1}(\alpha)z\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})\right)}.

Since

\hat{\varphi}(s)=1+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})

uniformly on
K
, and since

\frac{1}{A+B}=\frac{1}{A}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(\frac{B}{A^{2}}\right)

for
A=\gamma+z^{\alpha}
and

B=c_{1}(\alpha)z\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})
,
a first-order expansion of the reciprocal around
\gamma+z^{\alpha}
yields

\widehat{\mathcal{G}^{\tau}}(z)=\frac{1}{z(\gamma+z^{\alpha})}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-\alpha})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right),

uniformly for
z\in K
.
∎

B.10
Proposition
B.8

Proposition B.8

(Bounded-strip regularity of the continuous fractional Riccati solution)
.

Fix
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
such that

\Gamma_{\eta,R}:=\{\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi:\ |\xi|\leq R\}\subset\mathcal{S}_{T}.

Under Assumption
3.5
, the map
(t,z)\mapsto h(t,z)
is
jointly continuous on
[0,T]\times\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, each map

t\mapsto h(t,z)
belongs to
AC([0,T])\cap C^{1}((0,T])
, and there exists a
constant
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
such that, uniformly for
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
,

\displaystyle|h(t,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha},

\displaystyle 0\leq t\leq T,

(B.28)

\displaystyle|h(t,z)-h(s,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|t-s|^{\alpha},

\displaystyle 0\leq s,t\leq T,

(B.29)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}h(t,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1},

\displaystyle 0<t\leq T.

(B.30)

Moreover, the map
z\mapsto h(\cdot,z)
is locally Lipschitz from

\Gamma_{\eta,R}
into
C([0,T])
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof,
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
denotes a generic constant that may
change from line to line but depends only on
(\eta,R,T)
and the fixed model
parameters.

Let

F_{z}(x):=\frac{1}{2}(z^{2}-z)+\gamma(\rho\nu z-1)x+\frac{(\gamma\nu)^{2}}{2}x^{2},\qquad x\in\mathbb{C}.

Then the Volterra form of the fractional Riccati equation reads

h(t,z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds,\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

(B.31)

Since
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
is compact and

\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|h(t,z)|\leq M_{\eta,R,T},

the polynomial
F_{z}(x)
and its derivative in
x
are uniformly bounded on the
set

\{(z,x):z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R},\ |x|\leq M_{\eta,R,T}\}.

Hence there exists a constant
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
such that

|F_{z}(h(t,z))|\leq C_{\eta,R,T},\qquad 0\leq t\leq T,\ z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R},

(B.32)

and

|F_{z}(x)-F_{z}(y)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|x-y|,

(B.33)

for all
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
and all
x,y
with
|x|,|y|\leq M_{\eta,R,T}
.

We divide the proof into four steps.

Step 1: small-time bound.

By (
B.31
) and (
B.32
),

\displaystyle|h(t,z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}|F_{z}(h(s,z))|\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds=\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\Gamma(\alpha+1)}\,t^{\alpha}.

This proves (
B.28
).

Step 2:
\alpha
-Hölder continuity in time.

Fix
0\leq s<t\leq T
. Subtracting the two Volterra formulas gives

\displaystyle h(t,z)-h(s,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{s}\left((t-u)^{\alpha-1}-(s-u)^{\alpha-1}\right)F_{z}(h(u,z))\,du

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{\alpha-1}F_{z}(h(u,z))\,du.

Taking absolute values and using (
B.32
),

\displaystyle|h(t,z)-h(s,z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{s}\left|(t-u)^{\alpha-1}-(s-u)^{\alpha-1}\right|du

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{\alpha-1}du.

Since
\alpha-1\in(-1,0)
, the map
x\mapsto x^{\alpha-1}
is decreasing on

(0,\infty)
, and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\left((s-u)^{\alpha-1}-(t-u)^{\alpha-1}\right)\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(s^{\alpha}-t^{\alpha}+(t-s)^{\alpha}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}(t-s)^{\alpha}.

Also,

\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{\alpha-1}\,du=\frac{1}{\alpha}(t-s)^{\alpha}
.
Hence,

|h(t,z)-h(s,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|t-s|^{\alpha},

uniformly in
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
. This proves (
B.29
).

Step 3: continuity and Lipschitz dependence on
z
.

Fix
z_{1},z_{2}\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
and write

\delta h(t):=h(t,z_{1})-h(t,z_{2}).

Subtracting the two Volterra equations gives

\displaystyle\delta h(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{1}))-F_{z_{2}}(h(s,z_{2}))\right)\,ds.

Split the integrand as

\displaystyle F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{1}))-F_{z_{2}}(h(s,z_{2}))

\displaystyle=\left(F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{1}))-F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{2}))\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{2}))-F_{z_{2}}(h(s,z_{2}))\right).

By (
B.33
),

\left|F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{1}))-F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{2}))\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|\delta h(s)|.

Since
F_{z}(x)
is polynomial in
(z,x)
and
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
,
|x|\leq M_{\eta,R,T}
, there exists
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
such that

\left|F_{z_{1}}(h(s,z_{2}))-F_{z_{2}}(h(s,z_{2}))\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|z_{1}-z_{2}|.

Therefore,

|\delta h(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|z_{1}-z_{2}|t^{\alpha}+C_{\eta,R,T}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}|\delta h(s)|\,ds.

Set

u(t):=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|h(s,z_{1})-h(s,z_{2})|.

Then the previous estimate implies

u(t)\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|z_{1}-z_{2}|+C_{\eta,R,T}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}u(s)\,ds,\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

By the weakly singular Grönwall inequality, it follows that

u(t)\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|z_{1}-z_{2}|\,E_{\alpha}(C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha}),\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

Since
t\in[0,T]
, we obtain

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|h(t,z_{1})-h(t,z_{2})|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|z_{1}-z_{2}|.

In particular,
z\mapsto h(\cdot,z)
is locally Lipschitz into
C([0,T])
.
Combining this with (
B.29
) gives joint continuity of

(t,z)\mapsto h(t,z)
on
[0,T]\times\Gamma_{\eta,R}
.

Step 4: absolute continuity and derivative bound.

Define

g_{z}(t):=F_{z}(h(t,z)),\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

By (
B.29
) and the local Lipschitz property
(
B.33
),

|g_{z}(t)-g_{z}(s)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|t-s|^{\alpha},\qquad 0\leq s,t\leq T.

Thus
g_{z}\in C^{\alpha}([0,T])
uniformly in
z
, and

h(\cdot,z)=I^{\alpha}g_{z}
.
We now prove that
h(\cdot,z)
is differentiable on
(0,T]
.
Fix
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
and
t\in(0,T]
. Recall that

h(t,z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}g_{z}(s)\,ds,

where

|g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(r)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|s-r|^{\alpha},\qquad 0\leq r,s\leq T.

Let
h_{0}
be such that
|h_{0}|<\min\{t,T-t\}
, and let

u\in(-h_{0},h_{0})\setminus\{0\}
.

Case 1:
u>0
.

In this case,

\displaystyle\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,g_{z}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}g_{z}(s)\,ds.

Adding and subtracting
g_{z}(t)
, we rewrite this as

\displaystyle\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds+\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds\right].

(B.34)

For the bracketed term, a direct computation gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds+\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u}\int_{0}^{t+u}r^{\alpha-1}\,dr-\frac{1}{u}\int_{0}^{t}r^{\alpha-1}\,dr=\frac{(t+u)^{\alpha}-t^{\alpha}}{\alpha u},

and therefore

\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds+\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds\right]\to\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\,t^{\alpha-1}

(B.35)

as
u\downarrow 0
.

For the first integral in (
B.34
), fix
0<u<t/2
. By the mean value theorem,
for each
s\in[0,t)
there exists
\theta=\theta(s,u)\in(0,1)
such that

\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}=(\alpha-1)(t-s+\theta u)^{\alpha-2}.

Hence

\left|\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\right|\leq C_{\alpha}(t-s)^{\alpha-2},

and thus, using the
\alpha
-Hölder continuity of
g_{z}
,

\left|\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}(t-s)^{2\alpha-2}.

Since
\alpha>1/2
, we have
2\alpha-2>-1
, so
(t-s)^{2\alpha-2}\in L^{1}(0,t)
.
Moreover, for each fixed
s\in[0,t)
,

\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\to(\alpha-1)(t-s)^{\alpha-2}

as
u\downarrow 0
. Hence, by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\to(\alpha-1)\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds\qquad\text{as }u\downarrow 0.

(B.36)

For the second integral in (
B.34
), again by the
\alpha
-Hölder continuity of
g_{z}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{u}\int_{t}^{t+u}(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}(s-t)^{\alpha}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{u}\int_{0}^{u}r^{\alpha-1}(u-r)^{\alpha}\,dr=C_{\eta,R,T}u^{2\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{1}x^{\alpha-1}(1-x)^{\alpha}\,dx\to 0

(B.37)

as
u\downarrow 0
.

Combining the three limits above in (
B.35
), (
B.36
), and (
B.37
),
we conclude that

\lim_{u\downarrow 0}\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}=\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}t^{\alpha-1}+\frac{\alpha-1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds.

(B.38)

Case 2:
u<0
.

In this case,

\displaystyle\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,g_{z}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}g_{z}(s)\,ds.

Adding and subtracting
g_{z}(t)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\left[\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds-\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds\right].

(B.39)

For the bracketed term, another direct computation gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds-\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u}\int_{0}^{t+u}r^{\alpha-1}\,dr-\frac{1}{u}\int_{0}^{t}r^{\alpha-1}\,dr=\frac{(t+u)^{\alpha}-t^{\alpha}}{\alpha u},

so again

\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\left[\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\,ds-\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\,ds\right]\to\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\,t^{\alpha-1}

(B.40)

as
u\uparrow 0
.

For the first integral in (
B.39
), the same dominated convergence argument as above applies, because for fixed
s\in[0,t)
,

\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\to(\alpha-1)(t-s)^{\alpha-2},

as
u\uparrow 0
, and the same integrable domination by

C_{\eta,R,T}(t-s)^{2\alpha-2}
holds. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t+u}\frac{(t+u-s)^{\alpha-1}-(t-s)^{\alpha-1}}{u}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\to(\alpha-1)\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds,\qquad\text{as }u\uparrow 0.

(B.41)

For the second integral in (
B.39
), write
v:=-u>0
. Then

\displaystyle\left|-\frac{1}{u}\int_{t+u}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v}\left|\int_{t-v}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{v}\int_{t-v}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-1}(t-s)^{\alpha}\,ds=C_{\eta,R,T}v^{2\alpha-1}\to 0,

(B.42)

as
u\uparrow 0
.

Combining the limits in (
B.40
), (
B.41
), and (
B.42
),
we obtain

\lim_{u\uparrow 0}\frac{h(t+u,z)-h(t,z)}{u}=\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}t^{\alpha-1}+\frac{\alpha-1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds.

(B.43)

From (
B.38
) and (
B.43
), the right and left derivatives agree. Therefore
h(\cdot,z)
is differentiable on
(0,T]
, and

\partial_{t}h(t,z)=\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}t^{\alpha-1}+\frac{\alpha-1}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds.

(B.44)

It remains to estimate the derivative. Since
|g_{z}(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}
,

\left|\frac{g_{z}(t)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}t^{\alpha-1}\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1}.

For the integral term, using once more the
\alpha
-Hölder continuity of
g_{z}
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{\alpha-2}\left(g_{z}(s)-g_{z}(t)\right)\,ds\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{2\alpha-2}\,ds=C_{\eta,R,T}\frac{t^{2\alpha-1}}{2\alpha-1}.

Since
t\leq T
, we have
t^{2\alpha-1}=t^{\alpha-1}t^{\alpha}\leq T^{\alpha}t^{\alpha-1}
.
Therefore

|\partial_{t}h(t,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1},\qquad 0<t\leq T.

Finally, since (
B.44
) holds for every
t\in(0,T]

and
|\partial_{t}h(t,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\,t^{\alpha-1}
with
t^{\alpha-1}\in L^{1}(0,T)
(as
\alpha>0
), we have
\partial_{t}h(\cdot,z)\in L^{1}(0,T)
. Hence the representation

h(t,z)=h(0,z)+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{s}h(s,z)\,ds,\qquad 0\leq t\leq T,

which follows by integrating (
B.44
) from
0
to
t
and using the dominated convergence theorem as the lower limit approaches
0
, implies that
t\mapsto h(t,z)
is absolutely continuous on
[0,T]
.
∎

B.11
Lemma
B.9

Lemma B.9

(Regularity of the Riccati nonlinearity)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Proposition
B.8
. Fix
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
,
and define

g_{z}(t):=F_{z}(h(t,z))=\frac{1}{2}(z^{2}-z)+\gamma\rho\nu z\,h(t,z)+\frac{(\gamma\nu)^{2}}{2}(h(t,z))^{2}.

Then there exists a constant
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
such that, uniformly for

z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|g_{z}(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T},

g_{z}
is absolutely continuous on
[0,T]
, and

\displaystyle|g_{z}(t)-g_{z}(0)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha},

\displaystyle 0\leq t\leq T,

(B.45)

\displaystyle|g_{z}(t)-g_{z}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|t-s|^{\alpha},

\displaystyle 0\leq s,t\leq T,

(B.46)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}g_{z}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1},

\displaystyle 0<t\leq T.

(B.47)

Proof.

The bounds on
h
stated in Proposition
B.8
imply that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|h(t,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T},

and therefore the polynomial
F_{z}(x)
and its derivative in
x
are uniformly
bounded on the set

\left\{(z,x):\ z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R},\ |x|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\right\}.

Hence

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|g_{z}(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}.

The estimate (
B.45
) follows from

g_{z}(t)-g_{z}(0)=\gamma\rho\nu z\,h(t,z)+\frac{(\gamma\nu)^{2}}{2}\left(h(t,z)\right)^{2}

and Proposition
B.8
, which gives

|h(t,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha}
uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
.

Likewise, a direct application of the mean value theorem to the quadratic
polynomial
F_{z}
together with the Hölder estimate for
h
from
Proposition
B.8
yields (
B.46
).

Because
h(\cdot,z)
is absolutely continuous on
[0,T]
by
Proposition
B.8
, the chain rule gives

\partial_{t}g_{z}(t)=\left(\gamma\rho\nu z+(\gamma\nu)^{2}h(t,z)\right)\partial_{t}h(t,z),\qquad 0<t\leq T.

Using again the uniform bound on
h
and the derivative estimate
(
B.30
) from
Proposition
B.8
, we obtain
(
B.47
). Since

|\partial_{t}g_{z}(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1}
and

t^{\alpha-1}\in L^{1}(0,T)
, each
g_{z}
is absolutely continuous on
[0,T]
.
∎

B.12
Lemma
B.10

Lemma B.10

(Weakly singular quadrature constant)
.

For
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv

(B.48)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\zeta(\alpha)}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}=c_{1}(\alpha).

Moreover,

\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv\leq C_{\alpha}n^{-\alpha},\qquad n\geq 1.

(B.49)

Proof.

For
j\geq 1
,

\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv=j^{-\alpha}-\frac{(j+1)^{1-\alpha}-j^{1-\alpha}}{1-\alpha}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv=\sum_{j=1}^{m}j^{-\alpha}-\frac{(m+1)^{1-\alpha}-1}{1-\alpha}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)(1-\alpha)}+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}j^{-\alpha}-\frac{(m+1)^{1-\alpha}-1}{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}j^{-\alpha}-\frac{(m+1)^{1-\alpha}}{1-\alpha}\right).

Since

\sum_{j=1}^{m}j^{-\alpha}-\frac{(m+1)^{1-\alpha}}{1-\alpha}\to\zeta(\alpha)\qquad(m\to\infty),

we obtain (
B.48
). Moreover, for every
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle 0\leq\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv

\displaystyle=\int_{j}^{j+1}\left(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha}\right)\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\int_{j}^{j+1}j^{-\alpha-1}(v-j)\,dv\leq C_{\alpha}j^{-\alpha-1},

where we used the mean value theorem and the monotonicity of

x\mapsto x^{-\alpha}
. Summing over
j\geq n
yields

\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv\leq C_{\alpha}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}j^{-\alpha-1}\leq C_{\alpha}n^{-\alpha},

which proves (
B.49
).
∎

B.13
Lemma
B.11

Lemma B.11

(Weakly singular quadrature defect for
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
)
.

Let

\mathcal{G}(t)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(1-E_{\alpha}(-\gamma t^{\alpha})\right),\qquad t\geq 0,

and, for
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau)\,dv

(B.50)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau)\,dv.

Then

\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}=c_{1}(\alpha)\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n})+R_{n}^{\tau},

(B.51)

where

|R_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha},\qquad 1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

(B.52)

Proof.

Write

w_{n}(v):=\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau),\qquad 0\leq v\leq n.

Then (
B.50
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}=\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)w_{n}(v)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})w_{n}(v)\,dv.

We split

w_{n}(v)=w_{n}(0)+\left(w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)\right).

The contribution of the constant part
w_{n}(0)
equals

\displaystyle w_{n}(0)

\displaystyle\left[\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv\right].

By Lemma
B.10
,

\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv=c_{1}(\alpha)+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\alpha}),

and hence the constant part contributes

c_{1}(\alpha)\,w_{n}(0)+\mathcal{O}\!\left(n^{-\alpha}|w_{n}(0)|\right).

Since

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{\gamma}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t),

and

|f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t)|\leq C_{T}t^{\alpha-1},\qquad 0<t\leq T,

we obtain

|w_{n}(0)|=\frac{1}{\tau}|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n})|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-1}t_{n}^{\alpha-1}=C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-1}.

Therefore,

n^{-\alpha}|w_{n}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha},

since
n\geq 1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

It remains to estimate the oscillatory part

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{n}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\left(w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)\right)\,dv

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\left(w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)\right)\,dv.

We first record a derivative bound for
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Since

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{\gamma}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t)=t^{\alpha-1}E_{\alpha,\alpha}(-\gamma t^{\alpha}),

we may write

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\gamma)^{m}}{\Gamma(\alpha m+\alpha)}\,t^{\alpha m+\alpha-1},\qquad t>0.

For every
\delta>0
, this series and its termwise derivative converge uniformly
on
[\delta,T]
, so differentiation term by term is justified there and yields

(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{\prime}(t)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\gamma)^{m}(\alpha m+\alpha-1)}{\Gamma(\alpha m+\alpha)}t^{\alpha m+\alpha-2}.

The
m=0
term is of order
t^{\alpha-2}
, while the sum over
m\geq 1
is bounded
by
C_{T}t^{2\alpha-2}
. Since
2\alpha-2>\alpha-2
and
t\leq T
, we obtain

|(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{\prime}(t)|\leq C_{T}t^{\alpha-2},\qquad 0<t\leq T.

If
n=1
, the discrete sum is absent. Using the crude bound

|w_{1}(v)-w_{1}(0)|\leq|w_{1}(v)|+|w_{1}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}\left((1-v)^{\alpha-1}+1\right),\qquad 0\leq v\leq 1,

we immediately get

|\widetilde{R}_{1}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}=C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}1^{-\alpha}.

Hence it remains to consider
n\geq 2
.

For the integral over
[0,1]
, the mean value theorem and the derivative bound above give

|w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-2}\,v\,(t_{n}-v/\tau)^{\alpha-2},\qquad 0\leq v\leq 1.

Since
t_{n}-v/\tau\geq(n-1)/\tau\asymp n/\tau
for
n\geq 2
, it follows that

|w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-2}v.

Because

1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\in L^{1}(0,1),

we obtain

\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\frac{v^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\right)\left(w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)\right)\,dv\right|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-2}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha},

because
\alpha-2\leq-\alpha
for
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

For the discrete part, write

I_{j,n}:=\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\left(w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)\right)\,dv,\qquad 1\leq j\leq n-1.

We split the sum into the regions
1\leq j\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and

\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1\leq j\leq n-1
.

If
1\leq j\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor
, then for
v\in[j,j+1]
we have

t_{n}-v/\tau\asymp n/\tau
and
v\asymp j
. Hence

|w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-2}\,v\,(t_{n}-v/\tau)^{\alpha-2}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}j\,n^{\alpha-2}.

Using

\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv\leq C_{\alpha}j^{-\alpha-1},

we conclude that

|I_{j,n}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-2}j^{-\alpha}.

Therefore,

\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}|I_{j,n}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-2}\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}j^{-\alpha}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}.

If
\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1\leq j\leq n-1
, then for
v\in[j,j+1]
we use the crude bound

|w_{n}(v)-w_{n}(0)|\leq|w_{n}(v)|+|w_{n}(0)|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}\left((n-v)^{\alpha-1}+n^{\alpha-1}\right).

Moreover,
j\asymp n
on this range, so

\int_{j}^{j+1}(j^{-\alpha}-v^{-\alpha})\,dv\leq C_{\alpha}n^{-\alpha-1}.

Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}|I_{j,n}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha-1}\sum_{j=\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}\int_{j}^{j+1}\left((n-v)^{\alpha-1}+n^{\alpha-1}\right)\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha-1}\left(\int_{n/2}^{n}(n-v)^{\alpha-1}\,dv+n^{\alpha-1}\sum_{j=\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}1\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}.

Combining the bounds for the integral part and the two discrete ranges, we obtain

|\widetilde{R}_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha},\qquad 1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

Finally, combining the constant contribution and the oscillatory contribution,
we get

\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}=c_{1}(\alpha)\,w_{n}(0)+R_{n}^{\tau}=c_{1}(\alpha)\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n})+R_{n}^{\tau},

where

|R_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}.

This proves (
B.51
)–(
B.52
).
∎

B.14
Proof of Proposition
3.10

We split the proof into five steps.

Step 1: error equation.

By Lemma
B.5
, the discrete cumulative resolvent
satisfies, for every
n\geq 1
,

\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}=a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}_{n-k}^{\tau}+\tau^{-\alpha}\Phi_{n},\qquad\Phi_{n}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}.

(B.53)

On the continuous side, since

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(z)=\frac{1}{z(\gamma+z^{\alpha})}
,
we have

z^{\alpha}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(z)
,
and hence
\mathcal{G}
solves

D^{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(t)=1-\gamma\mathcal{G}(t),\qquad\mathcal{G}(0)=0.

(B.54)

Define

e_{n}^{\tau}:=\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}),\qquad 0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

Then

e_{n}^{\tau}=a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,e_{n-k}^{\tau}-L_{n}^{\tau},

(B.55)

where

L_{n}^{\tau}:=\mathcal{G}(t_{n})-a_{\tau}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})-\tau^{-\alpha}\Phi_{n}.

(B.56)

Step 2: defect decomposition.

Introduce the exact tail sequence

\bar{\varphi}_{j}:=\sum_{m=j+1}^{\infty}\varphi_{m},\qquad j\geq 0.

For the kernel from Definition
2.3
,

\bar{\varphi}_{0}=1,\qquad\bar{\varphi}_{j}=\frac{j^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)},\qquad j\geq 1.

(B.57)

Since
\varphi_{k}=\bar{\varphi}_{k-1}-\bar{\varphi}_{k}
and
\mathcal{G}(0)=0
,
summation by parts yields

\mathcal{G}(t_{n})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bar{\varphi}_{j}\left(\mathcal{G}(t_{n-j})-\mathcal{G}(t_{n-j-1})\right).

(B.58)

By (
B.58
) and (
B.57
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(t_{n})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau)\,dv+\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}j^{-\alpha}\int_{j}^{j+1}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau)\,dv.

(B.59)

On the other hand, from (
B.54
),

\tau^{-\alpha}\left(1-\gamma\mathcal{G}(t_{n})\right)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{n}v^{-\alpha}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n}-v/\tau)\,dv.

(B.60)

Using
a_{\tau}=1-\gamma\tau^{-\alpha}
and
\Phi_{n}=1-\bar{\varphi}_{n}
, we may
therefore rewrite (
B.56
) as

L_{n}^{\tau}=\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}+\gamma\tau^{-\alpha}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})\right)+\tau^{-\alpha}\bar{\varphi}_{n},

(B.61)

where
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}
is the quadrature defect from
Lemma
B.11
.

Step 3: bound the main defect.

By Lemma
B.11
,

\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}=c_{1}(\alpha)\,\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t_{n})+R_{n}^{\tau},

with

|R_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}
.
Since

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{\gamma}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t)\leq C_{T}t^{\alpha-1}
,
we obtain

|\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-1}+C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}.

(B.62)

Step 4: bound the lower-order terms.

By (
B.58
), (
B.57
), and the bound

|\mathcal{G}(t_{m})-\mathcal{G}(t_{m-1})|\leq\int_{t_{m-1}}^{t_{m}}|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(s)|\,ds\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}m^{\alpha-1},\qquad 1\leq m\leq N_{\tau},

we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{G}(t_{n})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}\left(n^{\alpha-1}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}j^{-\alpha}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}\right).

Splitting the discrete convolution at
j=\lfloor n/2\rfloor
gives

\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}j^{-\alpha}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}\leq Cn^{\alpha-1}\sum_{j\leq n/2}j^{-\alpha}+Cn^{-\alpha}\sum_{r\leq n/2}r^{\alpha-1}\leq C,

and hence,

\left|\mathcal{G}(t_{n})-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})\right|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}.

Since
\bar{\varphi}_{n}=n^{-\alpha}/\Gamma(1-\alpha)
for
n\geq 1
, it follows that

\left|\gamma\tau^{-\alpha}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}\,\mathcal{G}(t_{n-k})-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})\right)+\tau^{-\alpha}\bar{\varphi}_{n}\right|\leq C_{T}\left(\tau^{-2\alpha}+\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}\right).

(B.63)

Combining (
B.61
), (
B.62
), and
(
B.63
), we conclude that

|L_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}n^{-\alpha}+C_{T}|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-\alpha}n^{\alpha-1},\qquad 1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

(B.64)

Step 5: renewal amplification.

From (
B.55
), inversion by the renewal kernel gives

e_{n}^{\tau}=-L_{n}^{\tau}-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}L_{j}^{\tau}.

(B.65)

By Lemma
A.3
,

\psi_{m}^{\tau}\leq C_{T}m^{\alpha-1}

for any
1\leq m\leq N_{\tau}
.
Hence, using (
B.64
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}|L_{j}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}j^{-\alpha}+C_{T}|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}j^{\alpha-1}.

The first sum is uniformly bounded in
n
, while the second satisfies

\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}j^{\alpha-1}\leq Cn^{2\alpha-1}.

Since
n\leq N_{\tau}\leq T\tau
,

\tau^{-\alpha}n^{2\alpha-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}
.
Therefore,

\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}|L_{j}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{T}|c_{1}(\alpha)|\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}.

The same bound follows for
|L_{n}^{\tau}|
itself from (
B.64
). Combining this with (
B.65
), we obtain

|e_{n}^{\tau}|\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{res}}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},\qquad 0\leq n\leq N_{\tau},

where

C_{\mathrm{res}}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
Taking the supremum over
0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
proves
(
3.41
).

B.15
Proof of Proposition
3.11

Proof.

Define

(\mathcal{Q}_{\tau}g)_{n}:=\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}g(t_{j}),\qquad(\mathcal{Q}g)(t_{n}):=\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t_{n}-s)g(s)\,ds.

Recall from the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}
that

\mathcal{G}_{n}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}=\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\mathsf{S}_{n}^{\tau},\qquad n\geq 1,

and

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{\gamma}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t),\qquad t>0.

In particular,

|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(t)|\leq C_{T}t^{\alpha-1},\qquad 0<t\leq T,

and therefore,

|\mathcal{G}(t)-\mathcal{G}(s)|\leq C_{T}|t-s|^{\alpha},\qquad 0\leq s,t\leq T.

Using summation by parts, for
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
,

(\mathcal{Q}_{\tau}g)_{n}=g(0)\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}+\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}g^{\prime}(s)\,ds.

(B.66)

On the continuous side, integration by parts yields

(\mathcal{Q}g)(t_{n})=g(0)\mathcal{G}(t_{n})+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)g^{\prime}(s)\,ds.

(B.67)

Subtracting (
B.67
) from (
B.66
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q}_{\tau}g)_{n}-(\mathcal{Q}g)(t_{n})=g(0)\left(\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)\right)g^{\prime}(s)\,ds-\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)g^{\prime}(s)\,ds.

Hence,

\displaystyle\left|(\mathcal{Q}_{\tau}g)_{n}-(\mathcal{Q}g)(t_{n})\right|

\displaystyle\leq|g(0)|\,|\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)\right||g^{\prime}(s)|\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}|\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)|\,|g^{\prime}(s)|\,ds.

(B.68)

For the first term on the right hand side in (
B.68
), we insert and subtract
\mathcal{G}(t_{n-1})
:

|\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})|\leq|\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n-1})|+|\mathcal{G}(t_{n-1})-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})|.

By Proposition
3.10
and the

\alpha
-Hölder continuity of
\mathcal{G}
, this gives

|\mathcal{G}_{n-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n})|\leq C_{T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{res}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right).

We split

\left|\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)\right|\leq\left|\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n-j-1})\right|+\left|\mathcal{G}(t_{n-j-1})-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)\right|.

By Proposition
3.10
, the first term in (
B.68
) is
bounded by

C_{T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{res}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right).

Since
\mathcal{G}
is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous on
[0,T]
, the second term on the right hand side in (
B.68
) is bounded
by
C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}
uniformly for
s\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
. Therefore,

\left|\mathcal{G}_{n-j-1}^{\tau}-\mathcal{G}(t_{n}-s)\right|\leq C_{T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{res}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right).

Also
\mathcal{G}
is bounded on
[0,T]
, and thus the last integral in (
B.68
) is bounded by

C_{T}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}|g^{\prime}(s)|\,ds.

Using (
3.43
), we obtain

\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}|g^{\prime}(s)|\,ds\leq C_{g}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}s^{\alpha-1}\,ds.

If
n=1
, then

\int_{0}^{1/\tau}s^{\alpha-1}\,ds=\frac{1}{\alpha}\tau^{-\alpha}.

If
n\geq 2
, then
s\geq t_{1}=1/\tau
on
[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
, so

\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}s^{\alpha-1}\,ds\leq\tau^{-1}(t_{n-1})^{\alpha-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-1}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha},

since
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Therefore, uniformly for
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
,

\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}|g^{\prime}(s)|\,ds\leq C_{T}C_{g}\,\tau^{-\alpha}.

Combining the preceding bounds gives

\left|(\mathcal{Q}_{\tau}g)_{n}-(\mathcal{Q}g)(t_{n})\right|\leq C_{T}^{\prime}\left(|g(0)|+C_{g}\right)\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{op}}(\alpha)\left(|g(0)|+C_{g}\right)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

uniformly in
1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}
, where

C_{\mathrm{op}}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
This proves (
3.44
).
∎

B.16
Lemma
B.12

Lemma B.12

(Resolvent comparison for the continuous Riccati nonlinearity)
.

For every fixed
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
, there exists a coefficient

C_{g}(\alpha)\geq 0
and a constant
C_{\eta,R,T}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\left|\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(h(t_{j},z))-\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t_{n}-s)F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds\right|

(B.69)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{g}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

with
C_{g}(\alpha)\to 0
as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.9
, the functions

g_{z}(t):=F_{z}(h(t,z))

satisfy uniformly for
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
the bounds

|g_{z}(t)-g_{z}(0)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha},\qquad|\partial_{t}g_{z}(t)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha-1}.

Moreover,

g_{z}(0)=\frac{1}{2}(z^{2}-z)

is uniformly bounded on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
. Applying
Proposition
3.11
with
g=g_{z}
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\left|\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(h(t_{j},z))-\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t_{n}-s)F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{g}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

where

C_{g}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
This proves (
B.69
).
∎

B.17
Lemma
B.13

Lemma B.13

(Discrete weakly singular Grönwall on the
\tau
-grid)
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
T>0
, and
B>0
. Suppose that a nonnegative sequence

(a_{n})_{n=0}^{N_{\tau}}
satisfies

a_{n}\leq A+B\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}a_{j},\qquad 1\leq n\leq N_{\tau},

(B.70)

for some
A\geq 0
. Then

a_{n}\leq AE_{\alpha}(B\Gamma(\alpha)t_{n}^{\alpha}),\qquad 1\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

(B.71)

In particular,

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}a_{n}\leq C_{\alpha,B,T}A.

Proof.

Define iteratively

b_{n}^{(0)}:=A,\qquad b_{n}^{(m+1)}:=A+B\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}b_{j}^{(m)},\qquad m\geq 0.

Since the kernel is nonnegative,
(
B.70
) implies by induction on

m
that

a_{n}\leq b_{n}^{(m)},\qquad 0\leq n\leq N_{\tau},\quad m\geq 0.

We claim that, for every
m\geq 0
,

b_{n}^{(m)}\leq A\sum_{r=0}^{m}\frac{\left(B\Gamma(\alpha)t_{n}^{\alpha}\right)^{r}}{\Gamma(r\alpha+1)},\qquad 0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}.

(B.72)

The case
m=0
is immediate. Assume the claim holds at rank
m
. Since

\tau^{-\alpha}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}=\tau^{-1}(t_{n}-t_{j})^{\alpha-1}
,
and since
\alpha-1<0
while
r\alpha\geq 0
, for every

s\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
, we have

(t_{n}-t_{j})^{\alpha-1}t_{j}^{r\alpha}\leq(t_{n}-s)^{\alpha-1}s^{r\alpha}.

Since
\alpha-1<0
, the map
u\mapsto(t_{n}-u)^{\alpha-1}
is increasing on

[0,t_{n})
, while
u\mapsto u^{r\alpha}
is also increasing. Hence, for

s\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
,

\displaystyle(t_{n}-t_{j})^{\alpha-1}\leq(t_{n}-s)^{\alpha-1},\qquad\text{and}\qquad t_{j}^{r\alpha}\leq s^{r\alpha},

(B.73)

and multiplying the two inequalities in (
B.73
) yields

(t_{n}-t_{j})^{\alpha-1}t_{j}^{r\alpha}\leq(t_{n}-s)^{\alpha-1}s^{r\alpha}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}t_{j}^{r\alpha}

\displaystyle=\tau^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(t_{n}-t_{j})^{\alpha-1}t_{j}^{r\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}(t_{n}-s)^{\alpha-1}s^{r\alpha}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t_{n}}(t_{n}-s)^{\alpha-1}s^{r\alpha}\,ds=\frac{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(r\alpha+1)}{\Gamma((r+1)\alpha+1)}t_{n}^{(r+1)\alpha}.

Using the induction hypothesis in the definition of
b_{n}^{(m+1)}
yields

b_{n}^{(m+1)}\leq A\sum_{r=0}^{m+1}\frac{\left(B\Gamma(\alpha)t_{n}^{\alpha}\right)^{r}}{\Gamma(r\alpha+1)},

which proves (
B.72
). For each
fixed
n
, the sequence
m\mapsto b_{n}^{(m)}
is nondecreasing because the
kernel in (
B.70
) is nonnegative.
Moreover, (
B.72
) provides a
uniform upper bound independent of
m
, so
b_{n}^{(m)}
converges as

m\to\infty
. Since
a_{n}\leq b_{n}^{(m)}
for every
m
, passing to the limit and
using the monotone convergence of the truncated Mittag–Leffler series gives
(
B.71
). The final uniform bound
follows since
t_{n}\leq T
.
∎

B.18
Proof of Proposition
3.12

Recall from Lemma
B.3
that

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\tau}(z)

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\alpha}F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))+\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)

(B.74)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\tau^{-\alpha-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(H_{j}^{\tau}(z))+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z),

where

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-2\alpha}.

On the continuous side, Lemma
B.4
gives

h(t_{n},z)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t_{n}-s)F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds.

Set

e_{n}^{\tau}(z):=H_{n}^{\tau}(z)-h(t_{n},z).

Subtracting the two equations in (
B.74
) and (
B.24
), and inserting the intermediate discrete resolvent
operator applied to
F_{z}(h(\cdot,z))
, we obtain

\displaystyle|e_{n}^{\tau}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-\alpha}|F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))|+|\widehat{E}_{n}^{\tau}(z)|+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}|\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z)|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\tau^{-\alpha-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(H_{j}^{\tau}(z))-\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(h(t_{j},z))\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}F_{z}(h(t_{j},z))-\frac{1}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}f_{\alpha,\gamma}(t_{n}-s)F_{z}(h(s,z))\,ds\right|.

The last term is bounded by

C_{\eta,R,T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{g}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right)

by Lemma
B.12
. The explicit local term

\tau^{-\alpha}|F_{z}(H_{n}^{\tau}(z))|
is of order
\tau^{-\alpha}
because

H_{n}^{\tau}(z)
remains uniformly bounded on bounded strips, as follows from
Proposition
B.1
and the rescaling
(
3.25
). The same boundedness implies that
F_{z}
is
uniformly Lipschitz on the relevant range:

|F_{z}(x)-F_{z}(y)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}|x-y|.

Using

\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\tau^{-\alpha-1}=\frac{1}{\gamma\tau}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1-\alpha})
,
together with

\sum_{m\geq 1}\mathsf{S}_{m}^{\tau}=\tau
,
we absorb the coefficient mismatch into another
C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}

term and obtain

|e_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{g}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right)+\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}|e_{j}^{\tau}(z)|+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}|\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z)|.

Since

\sup_{1\leq j\leq n-1}|\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-2\alpha},\qquad\sum_{m\geq 1}\psi_{m}^{\tau}=\frac{a_{\tau}}{1-a_{\tau}}\lesssim\tau^{\alpha},

we have

\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}|\widehat{E}_{j}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-2\alpha}\sum_{m\geq 1}\psi_{m}^{\tau}\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}.

Hence

|e_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\left(\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{g}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right)+\frac{C_{\eta,R,T}}{\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}|e_{j}^{\tau}(z)|.

Finally, using the pointwise bound from Lemma
A.3
,

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathsf{S}_{n-j}^{\tau}=\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{a_{\tau}}\psi_{n-j}^{\tau}\leq C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}=\frac{C_{T}}{\tau^{\alpha}(n-j)^{1-\alpha}},

and setting

a_{n}:=\sup_{0\leq m\leq n}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|e_{m}^{\tau}(z)|,

we obtain from the preceding inequality that

a_{n}\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}+C_{T}\tau^{-\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(n-j)^{\alpha-1}a_{j},

where

C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
Applying Lemma
B.13
with

A=C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}

and
B=C_{T}
, we obtain

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}|e_{n}^{\tau}(z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}.

This proves (
3.45
).

B.19
Proof of Lemma
3.13

Proof.

By Proposition
3.12
,

\sup_{0\leq n\leq N_{\tau}}\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\left|H_{n}^{\tau}(z)-h(t_{n},z)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

where
t_{n}:=n/\tau
.
Using

\log\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)=\frac{\theta}{\mu}(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)\,H_{N_{\tau}-m}^{\tau}(z),

we write

\displaystyle\left|\log\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)-\log\phi(z,T)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\theta}{\mu}(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)\left|H_{N_{\tau}-m}^{\tau}(z)-h\!\left(\frac{N_{\tau}-m}{\tau},z\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\theta}{\mu}(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)\left|h\!\left(\frac{N_{\tau}-m}{\tau},z\right)-h\!\left(T-\frac{m}{\tau},z\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left|\frac{\theta}{\mu}(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)h\!\left(T-\frac{m}{\tau},z\right)-\log\phi(z,T)\right|.

(B.75)

For the first term in (
B.75
), Proposition
3.12
gives

\left|H_{N_{\tau}-m}^{\tau}(z)-h\!\left(\frac{N_{\tau}-m}{\tau},z\right)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}

uniformly in
m
and
z
. Moreover, Proposition
3.1
,
applied to the constant function
f\equiv 1
, yields

\frac{1-a_{\tau}}{\mu}\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)\leq C_{T}.

(B.76)

Hence the first term in (
B.75
) is bounded by

C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+{C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)}\,\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}.

For the second term in (
B.75
), note that

\left|\frac{N_{\tau}-m}{\tau}-\left(T-\frac{m}{\tau}\right)\right|=\left|\frac{N_{\tau}}{\tau}-T\right|\leq\frac{1}{\tau}.

Since
h(\cdot,z)
is uniformly
\alpha
-Hölder continuous on
[0,T]
by
Proposition
B.8
, we obtain

\left|h\!\left(\frac{N_{\tau}-m}{\tau},z\right)-h\!\left(T-\frac{m}{\tau},z\right)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}

uniformly in
m
and
z
. Using again (
B.76
),
it follows that the second term in (
B.75
) is bounded by

C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}
.

For the third term in (
B.75
), we apply Proposition
3.1
with

f(s)=h(s,z)
. By Proposition
B.8
, the function
h(\cdot,z)

is uniformly
\alpha
-Hölder continuous on
[0,T]
, and

|h(t,z)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}t^{\alpha},

uniformly for
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\theta}{\mu}(1-a_{\tau})\sum_{m=1}^{N_{\tau}}\hat{\mu}_{\tau}(m)h\!\left(T-\frac{m}{\tau},z\right)-\log\phi(z,T)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\mathrm{base}}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, where
C_{\mathrm{base}}(\alpha)\to 0
as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.

Combining the bounds on the three terms in (
B.75
), and enlarging

C_{\eta,R,T}
if necessary, we obtain

\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}}\left|\log\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)-\log\phi(z,T)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\log}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

where

C_{\log}(\alpha):=C_{\mathrm{Ric}}(\alpha)+C_{\mathrm{base}}(\alpha)
,
so that

C_{\log}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
This is exactly (
3.46
).
∎

B.20
Proof of Proposition
3.14

Proof.

For
z=\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, the triangle inequality gives

|\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)|=\left|\mathbb{E}\!\left[e^{zP_{T}^{\tau}}\right]\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\!\left[e^{\eta P_{T}^{\tau}}\right]=\Phi^{\tau}(\eta,T),

and similarly

|\phi(z,T)|=\left|\mathbb{E}\!\left[e^{zP_{T}}\right]\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\!\left[e^{\eta P_{T}}\right]=\phi(\eta,T).

Since
\eta\in\mathcal{S}_{T}
, both
\Phi^{\tau}(\eta,T)
and
\phi(\eta,T)
are finite,
and therefore the transforms are uniformly bounded on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
for all
sufficiently large
\tau
.

Now set

x:=\log\Phi^{\tau}(z,T),\qquad y:=\log\phi(z,T).

By Lemma
3.13
,

|x-y|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\log}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, with
C_{\log}(\alpha)\to 0
as

\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
. Using

e^{x}-e^{y}=(x-y)\int_{0}^{1}e^{tx+(1-t)y}\,dt,

we obtain

\displaystyle|\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)-\phi(z,T)|

\displaystyle=|e^{x}-e^{y}|

\displaystyle\leq|x-y|\int_{0}^{1}\left|e^{tx+(1-t)y}\right|\,dt.

Moreover,

\left|e^{tx+(1-t)y}\right|\leq e^{t\mathrm{Re}\ x+(1-t)\mathrm{Re}\ y}\leq\max\{|\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)|,|\phi(z,T)|\}\leq C_{\eta,R,T},

uniformly for
z\in\Gamma_{\eta,R}
and all sufficiently large
\tau
.
Therefore,

|\Phi^{\tau}(z,T)-\phi(z,T)|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{\Phi}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\eta,R}
, where one may take

C_{\Phi}(\alpha):=C_{\eta,R,T}\,C_{\log}(\alpha),

so that
C_{\Phi}(\alpha)\to 0
as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
This proves (
3.47
). The equivalent form
(
3.48
) is just the parametrization
z=\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi
.
∎

B.21
Proof of Proposition
3.16

Subtracting (
3.49
) and (
3.50
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{C_{R}^{\tau}(T,K)-C_{R}(T,K)}{S_{0}}=\frac{e^{-(\eta-1)k}}{2\pi}\int_{-R}^{R}e^{-\mathrm{i}\xi k}\frac{\Phi^{\tau}(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)-\phi(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)}{(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi)(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi-1)}\,d\xi.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{R}^{\tau}(T,K)-C_{R}(T,K)|}{S_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-(\eta-1)k}}{2\pi}\int_{-R}^{R}\frac{|\Phi^{\tau}(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)-\phi(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)|}{|(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi)(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi-1)|}\,d\xi.

By Proposition
3.14
,

\sup_{|\xi|\leq R}\left|\Phi^{\tau}(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)-\phi(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi,T)\right|\leq C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{2}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

where

C_{2}(\alpha)\to 0

as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle|C_{R}^{\tau}(T,K)-C_{R}(T,K)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S_{0}e^{-(\eta-1)k}}{2\pi}\left(C_{\eta,R,T}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{2}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)}\right)\int_{-R}^{R}\frac{d\xi}{|(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi)(\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi-1)|}.

Since
\eta>1
, the denominator does not vanish on the contour

\{\eta+\mathrm{i}\xi:\ |\xi|\leq R\}
, and the integral is finite for each fixed

R>0
. Absorbing the prefactor and the integral into the constants proves
Proposition
3.16
.

B.22
Corollary
B.14

Corollary B.14

(Uniform truncated pricing error on compact moneyness sets)
.

Fix
\eta>1
,
R>0
,
T>0
, and let
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathbb{R}
be compact. Then,
for all sufficiently large
\tau
,

\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|C_{R}^{\tau}(T,S_{0}e^{k})-C_{R}(T,S_{0}e^{k})\right|\leq C_{1}\tau^{-\alpha}+C_{2}(\alpha)\tau^{-(1-\alpha)},

(B.77)

for some constants
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}(\alpha)\geq 0
, independent of
\tau
, with

C_{2}(\alpha)\to 0
as
\alpha\uparrow 1^{-}
.

Proof.

The estimate (
B.77
) is uniform in
k
except for the
prefactor
e^{-(\eta-1)k}
. Since
k\in\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{K}
is compact,

\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}e^{-(\eta-1)k}=e^{-(\eta-1)\inf\mathcal{K}},

where
\inf\mathcal{K}
denotes the smallest element of the compact set
\mathcal{K}
, i.e.
\inf\mathcal{K}=\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}k
.
Taking the supremum over
k\in\mathcal{K}
in the estimate from
Proposition
3.16
yields (
B.77
).
∎
